CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWCHWANG, IITH OCTOBER 1901. SIR, I.-The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration having opened, on the 12th August i9oo, a Custom House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistie &amp;gt;f that trade from ISt July to 30th September 190. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Tariff rates and Likin at the rate of J1- per cenit. ad valorem on Imports, and f,- 1:r cent. ad i.alorem on Exports, with the exception &amp;gt;f the Duties on certain specified staples, which were reduced on ist May 1901 to the rate formerly assessed by the Native Customs. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. II. (,Received 14th October i90i.) CUSTOM HOUSEI KIAOCHOW, wrTH OCTonE ikl. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars NoS. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872,&#39;and 6 Of 1875, I ow transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1901. b. The Custom House ( 4 a) was opened on the ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import an4 Export Diuty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.9W 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and Likin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled by the Kiaochow Customs are known as Taputou ( ]J); Hungshihyai ( M ); Lingshanwei ji ), including Tachiangkou ( ); Nuku (A ); Tsangkou (Q Q); and Shatszkon ( p nluding Tengyao (E Z). c. The. word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means lo China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, th tatistics of.goods landed and consumed in German territory not being included. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 30, 1901.
232 CUSTOMTSGAZETTE. [JULY-SEPT., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 367. DISCOVERY OF A DANGEROUS REEF TO THE E.kSTWARD OF THE GREAT YANGTZE BANK.. THE following Hydrographical Note by Lieutenant- Commander W. 0. LYNE, commanding H.B.M.&#39;s surveying-vessel Waterwitch, is hereby circulated for general information: &quot;HYDROGRAPHICAL NOTE. &quot;H.M.S. Waterwitch, &quot;SHAnGHAI, ist August 1901. &quot;A coral patch on top of a volcanic pinnacle rising abruptly from the bottom lies in latitude 32&#39; 7&#39; I5&quot; N., longitude 125&#39; ii&#39; E.; it extends about -t mile in N.E. and S.W. direction, and is about 6o yards wide. The least depth obtained is I8 feet, but less water probably exists. &quot;From the S.W. extreme of the patch for a distance of j mile in a S.W. by S. direction extends a rocky ledge, the least water obtained on which was 17 fathoms. &quot;Very heavy overfalls and tide-rips were seen on the patch, and they extended about ) mile N.E. and i mile S.W. The shoalest spots are apparently at the extremes of the patch; at the N.E. extreme the sea was breaking oceasionally, and it would probably break all over the patch with a moderate sea running. &quot;The overfalls and tide-rips should be visible from a ship for at least a mile, and, with a favourable light, the light-green colour over the coral patch should be seen from the same distance. &quot;There are depths of 27 to 30 fathoms, sand and mud, at a distance of about - mile.all round the patch, except where the rocky ledge extends, so the lead will give no warning until within the limit where thle bottom becomes volcanic rock, scoria3, andlbroken coral. &quot;This patch is probably identical with &#39;Costa Rica&#39; Breakers, reported I868; &#39;Socotra Rock, June 19oo; &#39;Chiyuen&#39; Shoal, September i90o; &#39;Bombay&#39; Discoloured Water, October 1900. 47
4,r 44,7 1901.] NOTIFICATIONS. 233 &quot;Tidal streams run very strong over and in the vicinity of the rock, and they are of a rotatory nature; the stream was running south 2 knots an hour when the Watewitch was close to the patch. &quot;(Signed) W. 0. LYNE, &quot;Lieut.-Comimander.&quot; SHANGHAI, 2)l Agust 1901. &#39; No. 368. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN BUOYAGE OF NORTH CHANNEL. NoTICE is hereby given that, owing to the present unsuitableness of the North Channel for deep-draught vessels, the following buoys have been discontinued Shaweishan Bank Buoy, Midway Flat Buoy, Ch&#39;i-yao Bank Outer Buoy, and that the Drinkwater Point Buoy has been replaced by a ro-foot, conical, red buoy, surmounted by a spherical. cage. SHANGHAI, 28th Augu8 1901. 30 464

APPENDIX 1. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE, JULY-SEPTEMBER 1901. &amp;lt;32
25 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. *JULY-SEPT. (Received 22nd October 1901.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 12TH OCToBER 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1901. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan (* g), Lintin ( g) and Shamchun (@ J)4), on the north and west; Samun (2 IE), on the east; and Shaiichung (&amp;amp; j1 ) a Shatowkok (9 J ), on the 1nirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is called 5g f p. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. (Received 21St October Tgoi.)M n C USTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 14TH OCTOBER 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instruetions contained in your Circular NO. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1901. b. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are Malowchow ( ),Tungho ( and Chienshan ( ) with three sub-stations, Shekkok ( A), Kuttai ($ *), and Kwancbiap ( . c.
